   if anno_name.startswith(prefix) or anno_name.startswith(prefix + ',') or anno_name.startswith(prefix + '/'):\\n                return region\\n    return None\\n\\n\\ndef compute_firing_rates(spike_times, go_cue_time, bin_edges):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Compute firing rates for a single unit in a single trial.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        spike_times: array of spike times (absolute)\\n        go_cue_time: go cue time (absolute)\\n        bin_edges: bin edges relative to go cue\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        firing_rates: array of firing rates (Hz), shape (n_bins,)\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    # Align spike times to go cue\\n    rel_times = spike_times - go_cue_time\\n    # Only consider spikes in the window\\n    mask = (rel_times >= bin_edges[0]) & (rel_times < bin_edges[-1])\\n    rel_times = rel_times[mask]\\n    # Count spikes in each bin\\n    counts, _ = np.histogram(rel_times, bins=bin_edges)\\n    # Convert to firing rates\\n    bin_width = bin_edges[1] - bin_edges[0]\\n    return counts.astype(np.float64) / bin_width\\n\\n\\ndef get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, go_cue_time):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Find the tone onset time relative to go cue for a single trial.\\n\\n    The tone onset is the last sample_start before the go cue\\n    (accounts for early lick replays).\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    before = sample_start_times[sample_start_times < go_cue_time]\\n    if len(before) > 0:\\n        return before[-1] - go_cue_time  # negative value\\n    return None\\n\\n\\ndef extract_tongue_y_for_trial(tongue_timestamps, tongue_y, tongue_likelihood,\\n                                go_cue_time, bin_edges, likelihood_thresh=0.9):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extract tongue y-position in time bins for a single trial.\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        tongue_y_binned: array of mean y-positions per bin, shape (n_bins,)\\n            NaN for bins with no high-confidence tracking data\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    n_bins = len(bin_edges) - 1\\n    tongue_y_binned = np.full(n_bins, np.nan)\\n\\n    # Get absolute time window\\n    t_abs_start = go_cue_time + bin_edges[0]\\n    t_abs_end = go_cue_time + bin_edges[-1]\\n\\n    # Find tongue data in this window\\n    idx = np.searchsorted(tongue_timestamps, [t_abs_start, t_abs_end])\\n    ts_window = tongue_timestamps[idx[0]:idx[1]]\\n    y_window = tongue_y[idx[0]:idx[1]]\\n    like_window = tongue_likelihood[idx[0]:idx[1]]\\n\\n    if len(ts_window) == 0:\\n        return tongue_y_binned\\n\\n    # Align to go cue\\n    ts_rel = ts_window - go_cue_time\\n\\n    # For each bin, compute mean y from high-confidence points\\n    for b in range(n_bins):\\n        bin_mask = (ts_rel >= bin_edges[b]) & (ts_rel < bin_edges[b + 1])\\n        if not np.any(bin_mask):\\n            continue\\n        conf_mask = bin_mask & (like_window >= likelihood_thresh)\\n        if np.any(conf_mask):\\n            tongue_y_binned[b] = np.mean(y_window[conf_mask])\\n\\n    return tongue_y_binned\\n\\n\\ndef process_session(nwb_path, verbose=True):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Process a single NWB session file.\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        session_data: dict with neural, input, output, metadata for this session,\\n                      or None if session doesn't meet criteria\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if verbose:\\n        print(f\\\"Processing: {os.path.basename(nwb_path)}\\\")\\n\\n    f = h5py.File(nwb_path, 'r')\\n\\n    try:\\n        # === Extract trial information ===\\n        n_trials = len(f['intervals/trials/id'][:])\\n        outcomes = np.array([o.decode() if isinstance(o, bytes) else o\\n                            for o in f['intervals/trials/outcome'][:]])\\n        early_lick = np.array([e.decode() if isinstance(e, bytes) else e\\n                              for e in f['intervals/trials/early_lick'][:]])\\n        instructions = np.array([i.decode() if isinstance(i, bytes) else i\\n                                for i in f['intervals/trials/trial_instruction'][:]])\\n        auto_water = f['intervals/trials/auto_water'][:]\\n        free_water = f['intervals/trials/free_water'][:]\\n\\n        # Photostim info from trial table\\n        ps_onset_str = np.array([p.decode() if isinstance(p, bytes) else p\\n                                for p in f['intervals/trials/photostim_onset'][:]])\\n        ps_dur_str = np.array([p.decode() if isinstance(p, bytes) else p\\n                              for p in f['intervals/trials/photostim_duration'][:]])\\n\\n        # Go cue times\\n        go_cue_times = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps'][:]\\n\\n        # Sample start times (for tone onset calculation)\\n        sample_start_times = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times/timestamps'][:]\\n\\n        assert len(go_cue_times) == n_trials, f\\\"Go cue count ({len(go_cue_times)}) != trial count ({n_trials})\\\"\\n\\n        # === Load units ===\\n        classification = np.array([c.decode() if isinstance(c, bytes) else c\\n                                   for c in f['units/classification'][:]])\\n        anno_names = np.array([a.decode() if isinstance(a, bytes) else a\\n                              for a in f['units/anno_name'][:]])\\n\\n        # Filter by quality (classifier-based QC)\\n        good_mask = classification == 'good'\\n\\n        # Map brain regions and filter units with valid regions\\n        unit_regions = []\\n        unit_indices = []\\n        for i in range(len(classification)):\\n            if not good_mask[i]:\\n                continue\\n            region = map_anno_name_to_region(anno_names[i])\\n            if region is not None:\\n                unit_regions.append(region)\\n                unit_indices.append(i)\\n\\n        if len(unit_indices) == 0:\\n            if verbose:\\n                print(f\\\"  Skipping: no good units with valid regions\\\")\\n            return None\\n\\n        unit_indices = np.array(unit_indices)\\n        n_units = len(unit_indices)\\n\\n        # === Determine valid trials based on recording observation window ===\\n        # Units may only have data for a subset of trials if the recording\\n        # didn't span the entire behavioral session\\n        obs_intervals = f['units/obs_intervals'][:]\\n        obs_intervals_index = f['units/obs_intervals_index'][:]\\n\\n        # Find the minimum observation end time across all selected units\\n        min_obs_end = np.inf\\n        max_obs_start = -np.inf\\n        for ui in unit_indices:\\n            oi_start = 0 if ui == 0 else obs_intervals_index[ui - 1]\\n            oi_end = obs_intervals_index[ui]\\n            unit_obs = obs_intervals[oi_start:oi_end]\\n            if len(unit_obs) > 0:\\n                min_obs_end = min(min_obs_end, unit_obs[-1, 1])\\n                max_obs_start = max(max_obs_start, unit_obs[0, 0])\\n\\n        # Only include trials where the go cue (and surrounding window) falls\\n        # within the recording observation window\\n        recording_valid = ((go_cue_times + T_START) >= max_obs_start - 0.1) & \\\\\\n                          ((go_cue_times + T_END) <= min_obs_end + 0.1)\\n\\n        if verbose and np.sum(~recording_valid) > 0:\\n            print(f\\\"  Recording window limits: {np.sum(recording_valid)}/{n_trials} trials have neural data\\\")\\n\\n        # === Session filtering ===\\n        # Use ALL trials for session-level performance calculation (as in the paper)\\n        # Control trials: no photostim, no early lick, no auto_water, no free_water\\n        is_control = ((early_lick == 'no early') &\\n                      (auto_water == 0) &\\n                      (free_water == 0) &\\n                      (ps_onset_str == 'N/A'))\\n\\n        control_hits_left = np.sum(is_control & (outcomes == 'hit') & (instructions == 'left'))\\n        control_hits_right = np.sum(is_control & (outcomes == 'hit') & (instructions == 'right'))\\n\\n        # Performance on control trials (excluding ignore/no-response)\\n        control_responding = is_control & (outcomes != 'ignore')\\n        n_control_responding = np.sum(control_responding)\\n        if n_control_responding == 0:\\n            if verbose:\\n                print(f\\\"  Skipping: no responding control trials\\\")\\n            return None\\n\\n        performance = np.sum(is_control & (outcomes == 'hit')) / n_control_responding\\n\\n        if verbose:\\n            print(f\\\"  Performance: {performance:.1%}, Correct L/R: {control_hits_left}/{control_hits_right}\\\")\\n\\n        if performance <= MIN_PERFORMANCE:\\n            if verbose:\\n                print(f\\\"  Skipping: performance {performance:.1%} <= {MIN_PERFORMANCE:.0%}\\\")\\n            return None\\n\\n        if control_hits_left < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION or control_hits_right < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION:\\n            if verbose:\\n                print(f\\\"  Skipping: insufficient correct trials (L={control_hits_left}, R={control_hits_right})\\\")\\n            return None\\n\\n        # === Select trials ===\\n        # Exclude auto_water and free_water trials (as in reference code)\\n        # Also must have valid neural recording data\\n        trial_mask = (auto_water == 0) & (free_water == 0) & recording_valid\\n        trial_indices = np.where(trial_mask)[0]\\n\\n        if len(trial_indices) < 2:\\n            if verbose:\\n                print(f\\\"  Skipping: fewer than 2 valid trials\\\")\\n            return None\\n\\n        if verbose:\\n            print(f\\\"  Good units: {n_units}\\\")\\n\\n        # === Load spike times ===\\n        spike_times_data = f['units/spike_times'][:]\\n        spike_times_index = f['units/spike_times_index'][:]\\n\\n        # Build per-unit spike time arrays\\n        unit_spike_times = []\\n        for ui in unit_indices:\\n            start_idx = 0 if ui == 0 else spike_times_index[ui - 1]\\n            end_idx = spike_times_index[ui]\\n            unit_spike_times.append(spike_times_data[start_idx:end_idx])\\n\\n        # === Load tongue tracking ===\\n        tongue_ts = f['acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking/timestamps'][:]\\n        tongue_data = f['acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking/data'][:]\\n        tongue_y = tongue_data[:, 1]  # y-position\\n        tongue_like = tongue_data[:, 2]  # likelihood/confidence\\n\\n        # === Process each trial ===\\n        neural_trials = []\\n        input_trials = []\\n        output_choice = []\\n        output_outcome = []\\n        output_early_lick = []\\n        tongue_y_all_trials = []\\n\\n        for ti in trial_indices:\\n            gc = go_cue_times[ti]\\n\\n            # --- Neural data: firing rates ---\\n            fr_matrix = np.zeros((n_units, N_BINS), dtype=np.float64)\\n            for j, st in enumerate(unit_spike_times):\\n                fr_matrix[j, :] = compute_firing_rates(st, gc, BIN_EDGES)\\n            neural_trials.append(fr_matrix)\\n\\n            # --- Input: time from tone onset ---\\n            tone_onset_rel = get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, gc)\\n            if tone_onset_rel is None:\\n                # Fallback: use typical value\\n                tone_onset_rel = -1.85\\n            time_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel  # time since tone onset at each bin\\n\\n            # --- Input: photostim on/off ---\\n            photostim_binary = np.zeros(N_BINS, dtype=np.float64)\\n            if ps_onset_str[ti] != 'N/A':\\n                try:\\n                    ps_onset_abs = float(ps_onset_str[ti])\\n                    ps_dur = float(ps_dur_str[ti])\\n                    # Convert to relative to go cue\\n                    ps_start_rel = ps_onset_abs - gc\\n                    ps_end_rel = ps_start_rel + ps_dur\\n                    # Mark bins where photostim is on\\n                    photostim_binary = ((BIN_CENTERS >= ps_start_rel) &\\n                                       (BIN_CENTERS < ps_end_rel)).astype(np.float64)\\n                except (ValueError, TypeError):\\n                    pass\\n\\n            input_trial = np.stack([time_from_tone, photostim_binary], axis=0)  # (2, n_bins)\\n            input_trials.append(input_trial)\\n\\n            # --- Output: choice ---\\n            if instructions[ti] == 'left':\\n                choice = 0\\n            else:\\n                choice = 1\\n            output_choice.append(choice)\\n\\n            # --- Output: outcome ---\\n            if outcomes[ti] == 'ignore':\\n                outcome = 0\\n            elif outcomes[ti] == 'miss':\\n                outcome = 1\\n            else:  # hit\\n                outcome = 2\\n            output_outcome.append(outcome)\\n\\n            # --- Output: early lick ---\\n            el = 1 if early_lick[ti] == 'early' else 0\\n            output_early_lick.append(el)\\n\\n            # --- Tongue y-position (continuous, will discretize later) ---\\n            ty = extract_tongue_y_for_trial(tongue_ts, tongue_y, tongue_like,\\n                                           gc, BIN_EDGES, TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESH)\\n            tongue_y_all_trials.append(ty)\\n\\n        # === Discretize tongue y-position per session ===\\n        # Collect all valid (non-NaN) tongue y values across all trials in this session\\n        all_tongue_y_vals = np.concatenate(tongue_y_all_trials)\\n        valid_tongue_y = all_tongue_y_vals[~np.isnan(all_tongue_y_vals)]\\n\\n        if len(valid_tongue_y) > 0:\\n            p40 = np.percentile(valid_tongue_y, 40)\\n            p60 = np.percentile(valid_tongue_y, 60)\\n\\n            tongue_y_discrete_trials = []\\n            for ty in tongue_y_all_trials:\\n                ty_disc = np.ones(N_BINS, dtype=np.int64)  # default to middle category\\n                valid_mask = ~np.isnan(ty)\\n                ty_disc[valid_mask & (ty < p40)] = 0\\n                ty_disc[valid_mask & (ty >= p40) & (ty <= p60)] = 1\\n                ty_disc[valid_mask & (ty > p60)] = 2\\n                tongue_y_discrete_trials.append(ty_disc)\\n        else:\\n            # No valid tongue tracking - all middle category\\n            tongue_y_discrete_trials = [np.ones(N_BINS, dtype=np.int64) for _ in range(len(trial_indices))]\\n\\n        # === Build output arrays ===\\n \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Map a detailed Allen Brain Atlas annotation name to a high-level region category.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    if not anno_name or anno_name.strip() == '':\\\\n        return None\\\\n    for region, prefixes in REGION_MAPPING.items():\\\\n        for prefix in prefixes:\\\\n            if anno_name.startswith(prefix) or anno_name.startswith(prefix + ',') or anno_name.startswith(prefix + '/'):\\\\n                return region\\\\n    return None\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef compute_firing_rates(spike_times, go_cue_time, bin_edges):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Compute firing rates for a single unit in a single trial.\\\\n\\\\n    Args:\\\\n        spike_times: array of spike times (absolute)\\\\n        go_cue_time: go cue time (absolute)\\\\n        bin_edges: bin edges relative to go cue\\\\n\\\\n    Returns:\\\\n        firing_rates: array of firing rates (Hz), shape (n_bins,)\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    # Align spike times to go cue\\\\n    rel_times = spike_times - go_cue_time\\\\n    # Only consider spikes in the window\\\\n    mask = (rel_times >= bin_edges[0]) & (rel_times < bin_edges[-1])\\\\n    rel_times = rel_times[mask]\\\\n    # Count spikes in each bin\\\\n    counts, _ = np.histogram(rel_times, bins=bin_edges)\\\\n    # Convert to firing rates\\\\n    bin_width = bin_edges[1] - bin_edges[0]\\\\n    return counts.astype(np.float64) / bin_width\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, go_cue_time):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Find the tone onset time relative to go cue for a single trial.\\\\n\\\\n    The tone onset is the last sample_start before the go cue\\\\n    (accounts for early lick replays).\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    before = sample_start_times[sample_start_times < go_cue_time]\\\\n    if len(before) > 0:\\\\n        return before[-1] - go_cue_time  # negative value\\\\n    return None\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef extract_tongue_y_for_trial(tongue_timestamps, tongue_y,\\\\n                                go_cue_time, bin_edges):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Extract tongue y-position in time bins for a single trial.\\\\n\\\\n    Uses all tongue tracking data (no confidence filtering) to compute\\\\n    mean y-position per bin. NaN for bins with no tracking data.\\\\n\\\\n    Returns:\\\\n        tongue_y_binned: array of mean y-positions per bin, shape (n_bins,)\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    n_bins = len(bin_edges) - 1\\\\n    tongue_y_binned = np.full(n_bins, np.nan)\\\\n\\\\n    # Get absolute time window\\\\n    t_abs_start = go_cue_time + bin_edges[0]\\\\n    t_abs_end = go_cue_time + bin_edges[-1]\\\\n\\\\n    # Find tongue data in this window\\\\n    idx = np.searchsorted(tongue_timestamps, [t_abs_start, t_abs_end])\\\\n    ts_window = tongue_timestamps[idx[0]:idx[1]]\\\\n    y_window = tongue_y[idx[0]:idx[1]]\\\\n\\\\n    if len(ts_window) == 0:\\\\n        return tongue_y_binned\\\\n\\\\n    # Align to go cue\\\\n    ts_rel = ts_window - go_cue_time\\\\n\\\\n    # For each bin, compute mean y\\\\n    for b in range(n_bins):\\\\n        bin_mask = (ts_rel >= bin_edges[b]) & (ts_rel < bin_edges[b + 1])\\\\n        if np.any(bin_mask):\\\\n            tongue_y_binned[b] = np.mean(y_window[bin_mask])\\\\n\\\\n    return tongue_y_binned\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef process_session(nwb_path, verbose=True):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Process a single NWB session file.\\\\n\\\\n    Returns:\\\\n        session_data: dict with neural, input, output, metadata for this session,\\\\n                      or None if session doesn't meet criteria\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    if verbose:\\\\n        print(f\\\\\\\"Processing: {os.path.basename(nwb_path)}\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    f = h5py.File(nwb_path, 'r')\\\\n\\\\n    try:\\\\n        # === Extract trial information ===\\\\n        n_trials = len(f['intervals/trials/id'][:])\\\\n        outcomes = np.array([o.decode() if isinstance(o, bytes) else o\\\\n                            for o in f['intervals/trials/outcome'][:]])\\\\n        early_lick = np.array([e.decode() if isinstance(e, bytes) else e\\\\n                              for e in f['intervals/trials/early_lick'][:]])\\\\n        instructions = np.array([i.decode() if isinstance(i, bytes) else i\\\\n                                for i in f['intervals/trials/trial_instruction'][:]])\\\\n        auto_water = f['intervals/trials/auto_water'][:]\\\\n        free_water = f['intervals/trials/free_water'][:]\\\\n\\\\n        # Photostim info from trial table\\\\n        ps_onset_str = np.array([p.decode() if isinstance(p, bytes) else p\\\\n                                for p in f['intervals/trials/photostim_onset'][:]])\\\\n        ps_dur_str = np.array([p.decode() if isinstance(p, bytes) else p\\\\n                              for p in f['intervals/trials/photostim_duration'][:]])\\\\n\\\\n        # Go cue times\\\\n        go_cue_times = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps'][:]\\\\n\\\\n        # Sample start times (for tone onset calculation)\\\\n        sample_start_times = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times/timestamps'][:]\\\\n\\\\n        assert len(go_cue_times) == n_trials, f\\\\\\\"Go cue count ({len(go_cue_times)}) != trial count ({n_trials})\\\\\\\"\\\\n\\\\n        # === Load units ===\\\\n        classification = np.array([c.decode() if isinstance(c, bytes) else c\\\\n                                   for c in f['units/classification'][:]])\\\\n        anno_names = np.array([a.decode() if isinstance(a, bytes) else a\\\\n                              for a in f['units/anno_name'][:]])\\\\n\\\\n        # Filter by quality (classifier-based QC)\\\\n        good_mask = classification == 'good'\\\\n\\\\n        # Map brain regions and filter units with valid regions\\\\n        unit_regions = []\\\\n        unit_indices = []\\\\n        for i in range(len(classification)):\\\\n            if not good_mask[i]:\\\\n                continue\\\\n            region = map_anno_name_to_region(anno_names[i])\\\\n            if region is not None:\\\\n                unit_regions.append(region)\\\\n                unit_indices.append(i)\\\\n\\\\n        if len(unit_indices) == 0:\\\\n            if verbose:\\\\n                print(f\\\\\\\"  Skipping: no good units with valid regions\\\\\\\")\\\\n            return None\\\\n\\\\n        unit_indices = np.array(unit_indices)\\\\n        n_units = len(unit_indices)\\\\n\\\\n        # === Determine valid trials based on recording observation window ===\\\\n        # Units may only have data for a subset of trials if the recording\\\\n        # didn't span the entire behavioral session\\\\n        obs_intervals = f['units/obs_intervals'][:]\\\\n        obs_intervals_index = f['units/obs_intervals_index'][:]\\\\n\\\\n        # Find the minimum observation end time across all selected units\\\\n        min_obs_end = np.inf\\\\n        max_obs_start = -np.inf\\\\n        for ui in unit_indices:\\\\n            oi_start = 0 if ui == 0 else obs_intervals_index[ui - 1]\\\\n            oi_end = obs_intervals_index[ui]\\\\n            unit_obs = obs_intervals[oi_start:oi_end]\\\\n            if len(unit_obs) > 0:\\\\n                min_obs_end = min(min_obs_end, unit_obs[-1, 1])\\\\n                max_obs_start = max(max_obs_start, unit_obs[0, 0])\\\\n\\\\n        # Only include trials where the go cue (and surrounding window) falls\\\\n        # within the recording observation window\\\\n        recording_valid = ((go_cue_times + T_START) >= max_obs_start - 0.1) & \\\\\\\\\\\\n                          ((go_cue_times + T_END) <= min_obs_end + 0.1)\\\\n\\\\n        if verbose and np.sum(~recording_valid) > 0:\\\\n            print(f\\\\\\\"  Recording window limits: {np.sum(recording_valid)}/{n_trials} trials have neural data\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n        # === Session filtering ===\\\\n        # Use ALL trials for session-level performance calculation (as in the paper)\\\\n        # Control trials: no photostim, no early lick, no auto_water, no free_water\\\\n        is_control = ((early_lick == 'no early') &\\\\n                      (auto_water == 0) &\\\\n                      (free_water == 0) &\\\\n                      (ps_onset_str == 'N/A'))\\\\n\\\\n        control_hits_left = np.sum(is_control & (outcomes == 'hit') & (instructions == 'left'))\\\\n        control_hits_right = np.sum(is_control & (outcomes == 'hit') & (instructions == 'right'))\\\\n\\\\n        # Performance on control trials (excluding ignore/no-response)\\\\n        control_responding = is_control & (outcomes != 'ignore')\\\\n        n_control_responding = np.sum(control_responding)\\\\n        if n_control_responding == 0:\\\\n            if verbose:\\\\n                print(f\\\\\\\"  Skipping: no responding control trials\\\\\\\")\\\\n            return None\\\\n\\\\n        performance = np.sum(is_control & (outcomes == 'hit')) / n_control_responding\\\\n\\\\n        if verbose:\\\\n            print(f\\\\\\\"  Performance: {performance:.1%}, Correct L/R: {control_hits_left}/{control_hits_right}\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n        if performance <= MIN_PERFORMANCE:\\\\n            if verbose:\\\\n                print(f\\\\\\\"  Skipping: performance {performance:.1%} <= {MIN_PERFORMANCE:.0%}\\\\\\\")\\\\n            return None\\\\n\\\\n        if control_hits_left < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION or control_hits_right < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION:\\\\n            if verbose:\\\\n                print(f\\\\\\\"  Skipping: insufficient correct trials (L={control_hits_left}, R={control_hits_right})\\\\\\\")\\\\n            return None\\\\n\\\\n        # === Select trials ===\\\\n        # Exclude auto_water and free_water trials (as in reference code)\\\\n        # Also must have valid neural recording data\\\\n        trial_mask = (auto_water == 0) & (free_water == 0) & recording_valid\\\\n        trial_indices = np.where(trial_mask)[0]\\\\n\\\\n        if len(trial_indices) < 2:\\\\n            if verbose:\\\\n                print(f\\\\\\\"  Skipping: fewer than 2 valid trials\\\\\\\")\\\\n            return None\\\\n\\\\n        if verbose:\\\\n            print(f\\\\\\\"  Good units: {n_units}\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n        # === Load spike times ===\\\\n        spike_times_data = f['units/spike_times'][:]\\\\n        spike_times_index = f['units/spike_times_index'][:]\\\\n\\\\n        # Build per-unit spike time arrays\\\\n        unit_spike_times = []\\\\n        for ui in unit_indices:\\\\n            start_idx = 0 if ui == 0 else spike_times_index[ui - 1]\\\\n            end_idx = spike_times_index[ui]\\\\n            unit_spike_times.append(spike_times_data[start_idx:end_idx])\\\\n\\\\n        # === Load tongue tracking ===\\\\n        tongue_ts = f['acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking/timestamps'][:]\\\\n        tongue_data = f['acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking/data'][:]\\\\n        tongue_y = tongue_data[:, 1]  # y-position\\\\n\\\\n        # === Process each trial ===\\\\n        neural_trials = []\\\\n        input_trials = []\\\\n        output_choice = []\\\\n        output_outcome = []\\\\n        output_early_lick = []\\\\n        tongue_y_all_trials = []\\\\n\\\\n        for ti in trial_indices:\\\\n            gc = go_cue_times[ti]\\\\n\\\\n            # --- Neural data: firing rates ---\\\\n            fr_matrix = np.zeros((n_units, N_BINS), dtype=np.float64)\\\\n            for j, st in enumerate(unit_spike_times):\\\\n                fr_matrix[j, :] = compute_firing_rates(st, gc, BIN_EDGES)\\\\n            neural_trials.append(fr_matrix)\\\\n\\\\n            # --- Input: time from tone onset ---\\\\n            tone_onset_rel = get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, gc)\\\\n            if tone_onset_rel is None:\\\\n                # Fallback: use typical value\\\\n                tone_onset_rel = -1.85\\\\n            time_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel  # time since tone onset at each bin\\\\n\\\\n            # --- Input: photostim on/off ---\\\\n            photostim_binary = np.zeros(N_BINS, dtype=np.float64)\\\\n            if ps_onset_str[ti] != 'N/A':\\\\n                try:\\\\n                    ps_onset_abs = float(ps_onset_str[ti])\\\\n                    ps_dur = float(ps_dur_str[ti])\\\\n                    # Convert to relative to go cue\\\\n                    ps_start_rel = ps_onset_abs - gc\\\\n                    ps_end_rel = ps_start_rel + ps_dur\\\\n                    # Mark bins where photostim is on\\\\n                    photostim_binary = ((BIN_CENTERS >= ps_start_rel) &\\\\n                                       (BIN_CENTERS < ps_end_rel)).astype(np.float64)\\\\n                except (ValueError, TypeError):\\\\n                    pass\\\\n\\\\n            input_trial = np.stack([time_from_tone, photostim_binary], axis=0)  # (2, n_bins)\\\\n            input_trials.append(input_trial)\\\\n\\\\n            # --- Output: choice ---\\\\n            if instructions[ti] == 'left':\\\\n                choice = 0\\\\n            else:\\\\n                choice = 1\\\\n            output_choice.append(choice)\\\\n\\\\n            # --- Output: outcome ---\\\\n            if outcomes[ti] == 'ignore':\\\\n                outcome = 0\\\\n            elif outcomes[ti] == 'miss':\\\\n                outcome = 1\\\\n            else:  # hit\\\\n                outcome = 2\\\\n            output_outcome.append(outcome)\\\\n\\\\n            # --- Output: early lick ---\\\\n            el = 1 if early_lick[ti] == 'early' else 0\\\\n            output_early_lick.append(el)\\\\n\\\\n            # --- Tongue y-position (continuous, will discretize later) ---\\\\n            ty = extract_tongue_y_for_trial(tongue_ts, tongue_y, gc, BIN_EDGES)\\\\n            tongue_y_all_trials.append(ty)\\\\n\\\\n        # === Discretize tongue y-position per session ===\\\\n        # Collect all valid (non-NaN) tongue y values across all trials in this session\\\\n        all_tongue_y_vals = np.concatenate(tongue_y_all_trials)\\\\n        valid_tongue_y = all_tongue_y_vals[~np.isnan(all_tongue_y_vals)]\\\\n\\\\n        if len(valid_tongue_y) > 0:\\\\n            p40 = np.percentile(valid_tongue_y, 40)\\\\n            p60 = np.percentile(valid_tongue_y, 60)\\\\n\\\\n            tongue_y_discrete_trials = []\\\\n            for ty in tongue_y_all_trials:\\\\n                ty_disc = np.ones(N_BINS, dtype=np.int64)  # default to middle category\\\\n                valid_mask = ~np.isnan(ty)\\\\n                ty_disc[valid_mask & (ty < p40)] = 0\\\\n                ty_disc[valid_mask & (ty >= p40) & (ty <= p60)] = 1\\\\n                ty_disc[valid_mask & (ty > p60)] = 2\\\\n                tongue_y_discrete_trials.append(ty_disc)\\\\n        else:\\\\n            # No valid tongue tracking - all middle category\\\\n            tongue_y_discrete_trials = [np.ones(N_BINS, dtype=np.int64) for _ in range(len(trial_indices))]\\\\n\\\\n        # === Build output arrays ===\\\\n        output_trials = []\\\\n        for i in range(len(trial_indices)):\\\\n            out = np.array([\\\\n                np.full(N_BINS, output_choice[i], dtype=np.int64),       # choice (per-trial, broadcast)\\\\n                np.full(N_BINS, output_outcome[i], dtype=np.int64),      # outcome (per-trial, broadcast)\\\\n                np.full(N_BINS, output_early_lick[i], dtype=np.int64),   # How Trials Are Filtered (Quality Controls, Exclusion Criteria)\n\n**Session-level filtering** (per the methods text):\n- Performance > 65% on **control trials** (no photostim, no early lick, no auto_water, no free_water). Performance is computed as hits / (hits + misses) — i.e., among responding control trials, excluding ignores from the denominator.\n- At least 50 correct left-lick trials AND at least 50 correct right-lick trials (among control trials).\n\n**Trial-level filtering (what trials are included in the data):**\n- Exclude `auto_water == 1` trials (automated water delivery, not standard behavior).\n- Exclude `free_water == 1` trials.\n- Exclude trials where the go cue falls outside the neural recording observation window (determined from `obs_intervals`).\n- **Key decision:** Early lick trials and no-response (ignore) trials are NOT excluded from the decoder data — the agent explicitly reasoned that the decoder task requires these as outputs (early_lick and outcome including ignore), so they must be retained. This overrides the paper's own exclusion criterion for their analysis.\n- Photostim trials are also retained (photostim is a decoder input).\n\n**Recording window filtering (important discovery):** The agent discovered that some NWB files contain trials from multiple recording periods. For example, session `sub-440956_ses-20190208` has 480 rows in the trials table but `is_good_trials` is only 160 columns wide — the units only have valid data for the first 160 trials. The agent confirmed: `go_cue[159] < obs_intervals[-1,1] < go_cue[160]`. The fix was to filter trials where `(go_cue + T_START) >= max_obs_start - 0.1` AND `(go_cue + T_END) <= min_obs_end + 0.1`, using the common observation window across all selected units.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Neural Data Processing (Spike Sorting QC, Firing Rates, Binning)\n\n**Spike sorting QC:** The agent used the `classification` field from the NWB units table, filtering for `classification == 'good'`. This corresponds to the classifier-based QC described in the methods: region-specific logistic regression classifiers trained on 15 quality metrics, applied to label each cluster as 'good' or not. The paper's Kilosort2 output with classifier QC is already encoded in the NWB files. The agent did NOT apply any additional manual QC thresholds.\n\n**Additional filter:** Units with `anno_name` values that could not be mapped to any high-level region were excluded (i.e., empty or unrecognized annotation names returned `None` from the region mapping function).\n\n**Firing rate computation:**\n- Window: −2.5 s to +1.5 s relative to go cue\n- Bin width: 50 ms (non-overlapping)\n- Number of bins: 80\n- Method: `np.histogram(spike_times_relative, bins=bin_edges)`, then `spike_count / bin_width` (in seconds) → Hz\n- Bin edges: `np.linspace(-2.5, 1.5, 81)` (evenly spaced, 80 bins)\n- Bin centers used for input computations: `(edges[:-1] + edges[1:]) / 2`\n- Neural matrix per trial: shape `(n_neurons, 80)`, dtype `float64`\n\n**Spike time loading:** Spike times are stored as a single flat array in `units/spike_times`, indexed by `units/spike_times_index`. For unit `i`, spikes are at `spike_times[index[i-1]:index[i]]` (or `spike_times[0:index[0]]` for `i=0`).\n\n---\n\n### 4. How \"Choice\" Output Is Determined (trial_instruction vs Actual Lick)\n\n**Decision: The agent used `trial_instruction`, not the actual lick direction.**\n\nFrom the final code (step 85 and the full script text visible in the edit history):\n```python\nif instructions[ti] == 'left':\n    choice = 0\nelse:\n    choice = 1\n```\n\nThe variable `instructions` is loaded from `f['intervals/trials/trial_instruction'][:]`. The agent's reasoning (step 18) was to use `trial_instruction` as the \"choice\" because it represents the animal's instructed/intended direction. The task specification says \"Lick direction choice (left = 0, right = 1, per-trial)\" — the agent interpreted this as the instructed direction, not distinguishing between hit vs. miss. This means even for miss and ignore trials, `choice` reflects the instruction, not the actual lick.\n\nThere is no explicit discussion of whether to use the actual lick event vs. the instruction — the agent defaulted to `trial_instruction` without explicitly considering an alternative.\n\n---\n\n### 5. How Tone Onset Is Calculated\n\n**Key reasoning (step 24, reasoning_content at line 1689):**\n\n> \"The sample epoch has 3 tones (150ms each, 100ms ITI) = 3×150 + 2×100 = 650ms. Then delay epoch = 1.2s. So tone onset to go cue = 0.65s + 1.2s = 1.85s (matches!). But early lick trials can have replayed sample epochs, so the **last sample_start before go** is the correct tone onset.\"\n\n**Implementation:**\n```python\ndef get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, go_cue_time):\n    before = sample_start_times[sample_start_times < go_cue_time]\n    if len(before) > 0:\n        return before[-1] - go_cue_time  # negative value (e.g., -1.85s)\n    return None  # fallback: -1.85s\n```\n\nThe agent verified this empirically: go − last_sample_start ≈ 1.85 s across trials.\n\n**As decoder input (time-varying):** For each time bin at center `t` (relative to go cue), the \"time from tone onset\" input is:\n```python\ntime_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel\n```\nWhere `tone_onset_rel` is a negative number (e.g., −1.85). So at `t = −2.5`, `time_from_tone = −2.5 − (−1.85) = −0.65` (0.65 s before tone onset). At `t = 0` (go cue), `time_from_tone = 0 − (−1.85) = 1.85` (1.85 s after tone onset).\n\nThis is a **continuous, time-varying scalar** (not binary), representing elapsed time since the tone started — it varies per trial because different trials may have different tone onsets due to replays.\n\n---\n\n### 6. How Photostim Is Handled\n\n**Initial mistake (then corrected):** The agent initially treated `photostim_onset` from the trials table as an **absolute time** and computed `ps_start_rel = ps_onset_abs - go_cue_time`. This gave nonsensical large negative values (around −130 s) for photostim onset relative to the go cue.\n\n**Discovery of the bug (step 80, reasoning at line 5598):**\n> \"The photostim_onset in the trials table is NOT an absolute time — it seems to be a relative time (e.g., 1.94 seconds). But relative to what? Not the go cue, because then rel_onset would be around -0.5s, not -132s.\"\n\n**Verification (step 82):** The agent checked: `trial_start + photostim_onset = absolute onset`, and confirmed `absolute onset - go_cue ≈ −1.200 s` for all photostim trials. This matches the methods description: \"silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s)\" starting −1.2 s before go cue with 0.6 s duration (0.5 s + 0.1 s ramp-down).\n\n**Fix (step 85):**\n```python\nps_abs_onset = trial_start_times[ti] + ps_onset_val  # photostim_onset is relative to trial start\nps_start_rel = ps_abs_onset - gc   # relative to go cue\nps_end_rel = ps_start_rel + ps_dur\nphotostim_binary = ((BIN_CENTERS >= ps_start_rel) & (BIN_CENTERS < ps_end_rel)).astype(np.float64)\n```\n\n**Result:** The photostim binary input correctly marks bins where the laser is on (approximately −1.2 s to −0.6 s relative to go cue, entirely before the go cue). Photostim trials are included in the data (not excluded), with the binary input distinguishing them from control trials. The control trial check for session filtering uses `ps_onset_str == 'N/A'` to identify no-photostim trials.\n\n---\n\n### 7. How Tongue Y-Position Is Processed and Discretized\n\n**Data source:** `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` (side-view camera), field `data[:, 1]` = y-coordinate, `data[:, 2]` = DLC likelihood/confidence.\n\n**Initial approach (with confidence filtering, then changed):** The agent initially filtered tongue tracking data by `likelihood >= 0.9` before computing bin means. This caused a critical problem: only 14.1% of frames had high confidence, resulting in ~80% of bins having NaN values (no high-confidence data), and defaulting those NaN bins to category 1 (middle) caused extreme class imbalance (79% middle).\n\n**Revised approach (step 60–61):** The agent removed confidence filtering entirely and used **all tongue tracking data** regardless of likelihood:\n```python\ntongue_y_binned[b] = np.mean(y_window[bin_mask])  # no confidence filter\n```\n\n**Reasoning:** When using all data, computing `np.percentile(all_y, 40)` and `np.percentile(all_y, 60)` over the session guarantees a 40/20/40 split across valid time points by definition of percentiles. The trade-off is that low-confidence frames (tongue retracted/not visible) are included, which cluster around a narrow baseline value (p40=280.99, p60=281.24 when using all data). However this still produces a meaningful 40/20/40 split.\n\n**Discretization logic (per session):**\n1. Collect all non-NaN y-values across all trials in the session.\n2. Compute session-wide p40 and p60 of those values.\n3. For each time bin:\n   - If NaN (no tracking data): default to category 1 (middle).\n   - If `y < p40`: category 0 (low).\n   - If `p40 <= y <= p60`: category 1 (middle).\n   - If `y > p60`: category 2 (high).\n\n**Note:** After removing the confidence filter, NaN bins still occur only if there are literally no camera frames in that 50 ms window (rare). The discretized tongue_y is the only **time-varying** output variable.\n\n---\n\n### 8. How Tone Onset Is Calculated\n\n**Key reasoning (step 24, reasoning_content at line 1689):**\n\n> \"The sample epoch has 3 tones (150ms each, 100ms ITI) = 3×150 + 2×100 = 650ms. Then delay epoch = 1.2s. So tone onset to go cue = 0.65s + 1.2s = 1.85s (matches!). But early lick trials can have replayed sample epochs, so the **last sample_start before go** is the correct tone onset.\"\n\n**Implementation:**\n```python\ndef get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, go_cue_time):\n    before = sample_start_times[sample_start_times < go_cue_time]\n    if len(before) > 0:\n        return before[-1] - go_cue_time  # negative value (e.g., -1.85s)\n    return None  # fallback: -1.85s\n```\n\nThe agent verified this empirically: go − last_sample_start ≈ 1.85 s across trials.\n\n**As decoder input (time-varying):** For each time bin at center `t` (relative to go cue), the \"time from tone onset\" input is:\n```python\ntime_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel\n```\nWhere `tone_onset_rel` is a negative number (e.g., −1.85). So at `t = −2.5`, `time_from_tone = −2.5 − (−1.85) = −0.65` (0.65 s before tone onset). At `t = 0` (go cue), `time_from_tone = 0 − (−1.85) = 1.85` (1.85 s after tone onset).\n\nThis is a **continuous, time-varying scalar** (not binary), representing elapsed time since the tone started — it varies per trial because different trials may have different tone onsets due to replays.\n\n---\n\n### 6. How Photostim Is Handled\n\n**Initial mistake (then corrected):** The agent initially treated `photostim_onset` from the trials table as an **absolute time** and computed `ps_start_rel = ps_onset_abs - go_cue_time`. This matches the methods description: \"silenced ALM activity during the late delay epoch (last 0.5 s)\" starting −1.2 s before go cue with 0.6 s duration (0.5 s + 0.1 s ramp-down).\n\n**Fix (step 85):**\n```python\nps_abs_onset = trial_start_times[ti] + ps_onset_val  # photostim_onset is relative to trial start\nps_start_rel = ps_abs_onset - gc   # relative to go cue\nps_end_rel = ps_start_rel + ps_dur\nphotostim_binary = ((BIN_CENTERS >= ps_start_rel) & (BIN_CENTERS < ps_end_rel)).astype(np.float64)\n```\n\n**Result:** The photostim binary input correctly marks bins where the laser is on (approximately −1.2 s to −0.6 s relative to go cue, entirely before the go cue). Photostim trials are included in the data (not excluded), with the binary input distinguishing them from control trials. The control trial check for session filtering uses `ps_onset_str == 'N/A'` to identify no-photostim trials.\n\n---\n\n### 7. How Tongue Y-Position Is Processed and Discretized\n\n**Data source:** `Camera0_side_TongueTracking` (side-view camera), field `data[:, 1]` = y-coordinate, `data[:, 2]` = DLC likelihood/confidence.\n\n**Initial approach (with confidence filtering, then changed):** The agent initially filtered tongue tracking data by `likelihood >= 0.9` before computing bin means. This caused a critical problem: only 14.1% of frames had high confidence, resulting in ~80% of bins having NaN values (no high-confidence data), and defaulting those NaN bins to category 1 (middle) caused extreme class imbalance (79% middle).\n\n**Revised approach (step 60–61):** The agent removed confidence filtering entirely and used **all tongue tracking data** regardless of likelihood:\n```python\ntongue_y_binned[b] = np.mean(y_window[bin_mask])  # no confidence filter\n```\n\n**Reasoning:** When using all data, computing `np.percentile(all_y, 40)` and `np.percentile(all_y, 60)` over the session guarantees a 40/20/40 split across valid time points by definition of percentiles. The trade-off is that low-confidence frames (tongue retracted/not visible) are included, which cluster around a narrow baseline value (p40=280.99, p60=281.24 when using all data). However this still produces a meaningful 40/20/40 split.\n\n**Discretization logic (per session):**\n1. Collect all non-NaN y-values across all trials in the session.\n2. Compute session-wide p40 and p60 of those values.\n3. For each time bin:\n   - If NaN (no tracking data): default to category 1 (middle).\n   - If `y < p40`: category 0 (low).\n   - If `p40 <= y <= p60`: category 1 (middle).\n   - If `y > p60`: category 2 (high).\n\n**Note:** After removing the confidence filter, NaN bins still occur only if there are literally no camera frames in that 50 ms window (rare). Using `trial_instruction` means the choice is recorded as the instruction rather than the animal's actual behavior, which is problematic. Similarly, ignore trials have no choice at all. This conflates the instructed direction with the animal's actual decision.\n\n2. **Tone onset computation** - Uses the last `sample_start` before the go cue, which handles early lick replays reasonably well.\n\n3. **Tongue y-position** - No confidence filtering applied when computing percentile thresholds, so low-confidence tracking data gets included in the baseline calculations. This could skew the position estimates.\n\n4. **Trial filtering** - Keeps early lick, ignore, and photostim trials in the dataset.\n\n5. **Session filtering** - Requires >65% performance and ≥50 correct trials per direction, matching the methods.\n\n6. **Neural QC** - Applies classifier-based quality control, consistent with the reference.\n\n7. **Firing rates** - Computed in 50ms non-overlapping bins as specified, though the reference uses 100ms Gaussian bandwidth with 50ms stride.\n\n8. **Photostim** - Now correctly computed relative to trial start instead of absolute time.\n\n9. **Brain region mapping** - Maps to 14 categories using prefix matching on the annotation names.\n\nNow I need to reconsider the \"choice\" variable definition. The instructions specify lick direction as left=0 or right=1, but I'm using `trial_instruction` which tells what the animal was supposed to do, not what it actually did. For hit trials this works fine since the animal chose correctly, but for miss trials the animal licked the wrong direction, so using instruction would be incorrect. For ignore trials there's no choice at all. I should be using the actual lick direction the animal performed instead. ision models, the neural activity during the sample period encodes the instruction since that's what drives the preparatory response, so using trial_instruction as the decoding target makes sense from that perspective. Now I need to document the time_from_tone_onset calculations and verify the minimum values across sessions—the earliest bin center at -2.5 combined with tone onset timing should give me that -1.5 minimum I'm seeing. tone_onset_rel would be more negative, which explains why some sessions show a minimum of -1.5 instead of -0.6. When the last sample start occurs closer to the go cue due to early lick replays, tone_onset_rel becomes less negative, making the calculated time_from_tone values more negative overall. I'm noticing the verification output shows this pattern—most sessions have a -1.5 minimum while early sessions have -0.6, which aligns with this explanation. The code pulls from a global `sample_start_times` array for each trial, selecting the last sample start before the go cue—which works correctly since earlier trials' samples would be well before the current go cue, and multiple samples within the same trial would correctly identify the last one. So the -0.6 versus -1.5 split likely reflects different task parameters across mouse cohorts rather than a bug in the logic.\n\nDifferent mice probably have different delay periods. Rather than dig deeper into that, I should focus on what I can actually evaluate: the instructions in the reference files, the conversion notes, the agent's trajectory, the conversion code, and the verification output. Since the PDFs and original code directory aren't available in this environment, I'll need to rely on what the agent itself extracted from those sources and compare its decisions against the Notably, early lick trials, ignore/no-response trials, and photostimulation trials are all retained because they are needed as decoder outputs/inputs.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_mask = (auto_water == 0) & (free_water == 0) & recording_valid\n# ...\nrecording_valid = ((go_cue_times + T_START) >= max_obs_start - 0.1) & \\\n                  ((go_cue_times + T_END) <= min_obs_end + 0.1)\n```\n\niii. The AI noted in CONVERSION_NOTES: \"Excluded auto_water and free_water trials (as in reference code). Kept early lick trials (needed for early_lick decoder output). Kept ignore/no-response trials (needed for outcome decoder output). Kept photostimulation trials (needed for photostim decoder input).\" The recording window filter was added after discovering that some NWB files contain trials beyond the neural recording period.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `units/spike_times` (raw spike times) indexed by `units/spike_times_index` (ragged array index). Units are filtered by `units/classification` (quality control) and `units/anno_name` (brain region mapping).\n\nii.\n```python\nspike_times_data = f['units/spike_times'][:]\nspike_times_index = f['units/spike_times_index'][:]\nclassification = np.array([c.decode() if isinstance(c, bytes) else c for c in f['units/classification'][:]])\nanno_names = np.array([a.decode() if isinstance(a, bytes) else a for a in f['units/anno_name'][:]])\n```\n\niii. The AI documented that spike times are loaded from the NWB units table, using the ragged array format with index arrays to extract per-unit spike times.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are converted to firing rates using non-overlapping 50ms bins. For each unit and trial, spike times are aligned to the go cue, histogrammed into bins from -2.5s to +1.5s (80 bins), and divided by bin width (0.05s) to get rates in Hz. The result is a (n_neurons, 80) matrix per trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_firing_rates(spike_times, go_cue_time, bin_edges):\n    rel_times = spike_times - go_cue_time\n    mask = (rel_times >= bin_edges[0]) & (rel_times < bin_edges[-1])\n    rel_times = rel_times[mask]\n    counts, _ = np.histogram(rel_times, bins=bin_edges)\n    bin_width = bin_edges[1] - bin_edges[0]\n    return counts.astype(np.float64) / bin_width\n```\n\niii. The AI noted: \"50ms non-overlapping bins (as specified in decoder task). Spike counts divided by bin width (0.05s) to get rates in Hz. 80 time bins per trial.\" It acknowledged this differs from the reference code's 100ms bandwidth with 50ms stride (Gaussian-weighted) but follows the decoder task specification.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters are applied to units: (1) classifier-based QC requiring `classification == 'good'`, and (2) valid brain region annotation (anno_name must map to one of the 14 predefined region categories). Units that fail either filter are excluded. Additionally, the recording observation window is checked to ensure all selected units have data for the included trials.\n\nii.\n```python\ngood_mask = classification == 'good'\nfor i in range(len(classification)):\n    if not good_mask[i]:\n        continue\n    region = map_anno_name_to_region(anno_names[i])\n    if region is not None:\n        unit_regions.append(region)\n        unit_indices.append(i)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented: \"Classifier-based QC (classification='good' in NWB units table). The reference code uses qc_mode='classifier'.\" and \"Units with empty or unmappable annotation names excluded (490 units, <1%).\" Final count: 57,925 good units across 144 sessions.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to the go cue onset (t=0). Spike times are subtracted by the go cue time to get relative times, then histogrammed into bins from -2.5s to +1.5s relative to the go cue.\n\nii.\n```python\nrel_times = spike_times - go_cue_time\n# ...\nBIN_EDGES = np.linspace(T_START, T_END, N_BINS + 1)  # T_START=-2.5, T_END=1.5\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES state: \"Aligned to go cue onset (t=0). Window: -2.5s to +1.5s (as specified in decoder task).\"\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The temporal resolution is 50ms (0.05s) bins. There are 80 bins spanning -2.5s to +1.5s. No rebinning is applied; firing rates are computed directly from spike times into these bins using `np.histogram`.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_WIDTH = 0.05  # 50 ms bins\nT_START = -2.5\nT_END = 1.5\nN_BINS = int((T_END - T_START) / BIN_WIDTH)  # 80 bins\nBIN_EDGES = np.linspace(T_START, T_END, N_BINS + 1)\n```\n\niii. The AI noted this follows the decoder task specification (\"Use 50-ms-width bins for computing firing rates\") and differs from the reference code's 100ms bandwidth with 50ms stride.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times/timestamps` (tone onset times) and `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps` (go cue times). The last sample start time before the go cue is used as the tone onset for each trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nsample_start_times = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times/timestamps'][:]\n# ...\ndef get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, go_cue_time):\n    before = sample_start_times[sample_start_times < go_cue_time]\n    if len(before) > 0:\n        return before[-1] - go_cue_time\n    return None\n```\n\niii. The AI reasoned in the trajectory: \"The sample epoch has 3 tones (150ms each, 100ms ITI) = 650ms. Then delay epoch = 1.2s. So tone onset to go cue = 1.85s. But early lick trials can have replayed sample epochs, so the last sample_start before go is the correct tone onset.\"\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds*?\n\ni. For each trial, the last `sample_start_time` before the go cue is found and converted to a time relative to the go cue (a negative value, typically around -1.85s). Then for each time bin, the time from tone onset is computed as `BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel`, giving a continuous time-varying signal representing elapsed time since tone onset. If no sample_start is found before the go cue, a fallback value of -1.85s is used.\n\nii.\n```python\ntone_onset_rel = get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, gc)\nif tone_onset_rel is None:\n    tone_onset_rel = -1.85\ntime_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel  # time since tone onset at each bin\n```\n\niii. Typical value at go cue: ~1.85s.\"\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Time from tone onset is computed at the same bin centers as the neural data, ensuring temporal alignment. Both use `BIN_CENTERS = (BIN_EDGES[:-1] + BIN_EDGES[1:]) / 2`, which are the midpoints of the 80 time bins from -2.5s to +1.5s relative to the go cue.\n\nii.\n```python\ntime_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel  # shape: (80,), aligned with neural bins\ninput_trial = np.stack([time_from_tone, photostim_binary], axis=0)  # (2, 80)\n```\n\niii. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Photostimulation*?\n\ni. For each trial with photostim (onset != 'N/A'): (1) convert onset from trial-relative to absolute time by adding trial start, (2) convert to go-cue-relative by subtracting go cue time, (3) compute end time as onset + duration, (4) create a binary array marking which time bins have their center within the photostim window. The unique subjects are collected into a sorted list, and each session is assigned an index into this list via `subject_idx`.\n\nii.\n```python\nbasename = os.path.basename(nwb_path)\nsubject_id = basename.split('_ses-')[0].replace('sub-', '')\n# ...\nunique_subjects = sorted(set(all_subject_ids))\nsubject_idx = np.array([unique_subjects.index(s) for s in all_subject_ids])\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES mentions 28 subjects. The verification output confirms 28 subjects with varying numbers of sessions per subject (e.g., Subject 440956: 3 sessions, Subject 480927: 10 sessions).\n\n## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. Each NWB file corresponds to one recording session. Sessions that pass quality filters are included in the final dataset. The AI applies session-level filtering: performance > 65% on control trials, and at least 50 correct left + 50 correct right trials. Sessions with no good units with valid brain regions are also excluded. Result: 144 of 174 sessions kept.\n\nii.\n```python\nif performance <= MIN_PERFORMANCE:\n    return None\nif control_hits_left < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION or control_hits_right < MIN_CORRECT_PER_DIRECTION:\n    return None\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES states: \"144 sessions kept, 30 skipped\" with reasons including low performance (20), insufficient correct trials per direction (7), no good units (1), and other (2). This matches the methods text criteria.\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. Trials are read from the `intervals/trials` table in each NWB file. The total trial count per session is determined by the length of `intervals/trials/id`. Each trial is associated with a go cue time from `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps`. Trials are filtered based on quality criteria (see 1-e).\n\nii.\n```python\nn_trials = len(f['intervals/trials/id'][:])\ngo_cue_times = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps'][:]\nassert len(go_cue_times) == n_trials\n```\n\niii. The AI verified that go cue counts match trial counts. The final dataset contains 74,759 trials across 144 sessions, with a mean of ~519 trials per session.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. The AI applies three trial-level filters: (1) exclude `auto_water` trials, (2) exclude `free_water` trials, (3) exclude trials where the neural recording window doesn't cover the full analysis time window around the go cue. Notably, early lick trials, ignore/no-response trials, and photostimulation trials are all retained because they are needed as decoder outputs/inputs.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_mask = (auto_water == 0) & (free_water == 0) & recording_valid\n# ...\nrecording_valid = ((go_cue_times + T_START) >= max_obs_start - 0.1) & \\\n                  ((go_cue_times + T_END) <= min_obs_end + 0.1)\n```\n\niii. The AI noted in CONVERSION_NOTES: \"Excluded auto_water and free_water trials (as in reference code). Kept early lick trials (needed for early_lick decoder output). Kept ignore/no-response trials (needed for outcome decoder output). Kept photostimulation trials (needed for photostim decoder input).\" The recording window filter was added after discovering that some NWB files contain trials beyond the neural recording period.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `units/spike_times` (raw spike times) indexed by `units/spike_times_index` (ragged array index). Units are filtered by `units/classification` (quality control) and `units/anno_name` (brain region mapping).\n\nii.\n```python\nspike_times_data = f['units/spike_times'][:]\nspike_times_index = f['units/spike_times_index'][:]\nclassification = np.array([c.decode() if isinstance(c, bytes) else c for c in f['units/classification'][:]])\nanno_names = np.array([a.decode() if isinstance(a, bytes) else a for a in f['units/anno_name'][:]])\n```\n\niii. The AI documented that spike times are loaded from the NWB units table, using the ragged array format with index arrays to extract per-unit spike times.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are converted to firing rates using non-overlapping 50ms bins. For each unit and trial, spike times are aligned to the go cue, histogrammed into bins from -2.5s to +1.5s (80 bins), and divided by bin width (0.05s) to get rates in Hz. The result is a (n_neurons, 80) matrix per trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_firing_rates(spike_times, go_cue_time, bin_edges):\n    rel_times = spike_times - go_cue_time\n    mask = (rel_times >= bin_edges[0]) & (rel_times < bin_edges[-1])\n    rel_times = rel_times[mask]\n    counts, _ = np.histogram(rel_times, bins=bin_edges)\n    bin_width = bin_edges[1] - bin_edges[0]\n    return counts.astype(np.float64) / bin_width\n```\n\niii. The AI noted: \"50ms non-overlapping bins (as specified in decoder task). Spike counts divided by bin width (0.05s) to get rates in Hz. 80 time bins per trial.\" It acknowledged this differs from the reference code's 100ms bandwidth with 50ms stride (Gaussian-weighted) but follows the decoder task specification.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Two filters are applied to units: (1) classifier-based QC requiring `classification == 'good'`, and (2) valid brain region annotation (anno_name must map to one of the 14 predefined region categories). Units that fail either filter are excluded. Additionally, the recording observation window is checked to ensure all selected units have data for the included trials.\n\nii.\n```python\ngood_mask = classification == 'good'\nfor i in range(len(classification)):\n    if not good_mask[i]:\n        continue\n    region = map_anno_name_to_region(anno_names[i])\n    if region is not None:\n        unit_regions.append(region)\n        unit_indices.append(i)\n```\n\niii. The AI documented: \"Classifier-based QC (classification='good' in NWB units table). The reference code uses qc_mode='classifier'.\" and \"Units with empty or unmappable annotation names excluded (490 units, <1%).\" Final count: 57,925 good units across 144 sessions.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Neural data is aligned to the go cue onset (t=0). Spike times are subtracted by the go cue time to get relative times, then histogrammed into bins from -2.5s to +1.5s relative to the go cue.\n\nii.\n```python\nrel_times = spike_times - go_cue_time\n# ...\nBIN_EDGES = np.linspace(T_START, T_END, N_BINS + 1)  # T_START=-2.5, T_END=1.5\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES state: \"Aligned to go cue onset (t=0). Window: -2.5s to +1.5s (as specified in decoder task).\"\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. The temporal resolution is 50ms (0.05s) bins. There are 80 bins spanning -2.5s to +1.5s. No rebinning is applied; firing rates are computed directly from spike times into these bins using `np.histogram`.\n\nii.\n```python\nBIN_WIDTH = 0.05  # 50 ms bins\nT_START = -2.5\nT_END = 1.5\nN_BINS = int((T_END - T_START) / BIN_WIDTH)  # 80 bins\nBIN_EDGES = np.linspace(T_START, T_END, N_BINS + 1)\n```\n\niii. The AI noted this follows the decoder task specification (\"Use 50-ms-width bins for computing firing rates\") and differs from the reference code's 100ms bandwidth with 50ms stride.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times/timestamps` (tone onset times) and `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/go_start_times/timestamps` (go cue times). The last sample start time before the go cue is used as the tone onset for each trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nsample_start_times = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times/timestamps'][:]\n# ...\ndef get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, go_cue_time):\n    before = sample_start_times[sample_start_times < go_cue_time]\n    if len(before) > 0:\n        return before[-1] - go_cue_time\n    return None\n```\n\niii. The AI reasoned in the trajectory: \"The sample epoch has 3 tones (150ms each, 100ms ITI) = 650ms. Then delay epoch = 1.2s. So tone onset to go cue = 1.85s. But early lick trials can have replayed sample epochs, so the last sample_start before go is the correct tone onset.\"\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds*?\n\ni. For each trial, the last `sample_start_time` before the go cue is found and converted to a time relative to the go cue (a negative value, typically around -1.85s). Then for each time bin, the time from tone onset is computed as `BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel`, giving a continuous time-varying signal representing elapsed time since tone onset. If no sample_start is found before the go cue, a fallback value of -1.85s is used.\n\nii.\n```python\ntone_onset_rel = get_tone_onset_relative_to_go(sample_start_times, gc)\nif tone_onset_rel is None:\n    tone_onset_rel = -1.85\ntime_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel  # time since tone onset at each bin\n```\n\niii. The AI documented: \"Computed as seconds since the first tone of the (successful) sample epoch. For trials with early lick replays, uses the last sample_start before the go cue. Typical value at go cue: ~1.85s.\"\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Time from tone onset is computed at the same bin centers as the neural data, ensuring temporal alignment. Both use `BIN_CENTERS = (BIN_EDGES[:-1] + BIN_EDGES[1:]) / 2`, which are the midpoints of the 80 time bins from -2.5s to +1.5s relative to the go cue.\n\nii.\n```python\ntime_from_tone = BIN_CENTERS - tone_onset_rel  # shape: (80,), aligned with neural bins\ninput_trial = np.stack([time_from_tone, photostim_binary], axis=0)  # (2, 80)\n```\n\niii. The alignment is implicit through the shared use of `BIN_CENTERS` for both neural and input computations.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Photostimulation* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from three NWB fields: `intervals/trials/photostim_onset` (onset time relative to trial start, or 'N/A'), `intervals/trials/photostim_duration` (duration of stimulation), and `intervals/trials/start_time` (trial start time, needed to convert onset to absolute time).\n\nii.\n```python\nps_onset_str = np.array([p.decode() if isinstance(p, bytes) else p\n                        for p in f['intervals/trials/photostim_onset'][:]])\nps_dur_str = np.array([p.decode() if isinstance(p, bytes) else p\n                      for p in f['intervals/trials/photostim_duration'][:]])\ntrial_start_times = f['intervals/trials/start_time'][:]\n```\n\niii. The AI discovered during development that `photostim_onset` is relative to trial start (not absolute), which required also loading `trial_start_times`.\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Photostimulation*?\n\ni. For each trial with photostim (onset != 'N/A'): (1) convert onset from trial-relative to absolute time by adding trial start, (2) convert to go-cue-relative by subtracting go cue time, (3) compute end time as onset + duration, (4) create a binary array marking which time bins have their center within the photostim window. For non-photostim trials, the array is all zeros.\n\nii.\n```python\nif ps_onset_str[ti] != 'N/A':\n    ps_onset_val = float(ps_onset_str[ti])\n    ps_dur = float(ps_dur_str[ti])\n    ps_abs_onset = trial_start_times[ti] + ps_onset_val\n    ps_start_rel = ps_abs_onset - gc\n    ps_end_rel = ps_start_rel + ps_dur\n    photostim_binary = ((BIN_CENTERS >= ps_start_rel) &\n                       (BIN_CENTERS < ps_end_rel)).astype(np.float64)\n```\n\niii. The AI initially treated photostim_onset as absolute time, producing nonsensical values (~-132s relative to go cue). After investigation, the agent confirmed `trial_start + photostim_onset = absolute onset` and verified `rel_to_go ~ -1.2s`, matching the methods description of late delay epoch silencing.\n\n## 4-c. How is the `input` *Photostimulation* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The photostim binary signal is computed at the same `BIN_CENTERS` as the neural data, ensuring temporal alignment. The result is a binary (0/1) array of shape (80,), with 1s marking bins where photostim is active. Photostim typically falls around -1.2s to -0.6s relative to go cue.\n\nii.\n```python\nphotostim_binary = ((BIN_CENTERS >= ps_start_rel) & (BIN_CENTERS < ps_end_rel)).astype(np.float64)\n```\n\niii. Aligned through shared `BIN_CENTERS` array, consistent with neural data binning.\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick direction choice* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `intervals/trials/trial_instruction` which encodes the instructed lick direction ('left' or 'right') for each trial. This is the direction the animal was supposed to lick, not necessarily the actual lick direction.\n\nii.\n```python\ninstructions = np.array([i.decode() if isinstance(i, bytes) else i\n                        for i in f['intervals/trials/trial_instruction'][:]])\n# ...\nif instructions[ti] == 'left':\n    choice = 0\nelse:\n    choice = 1\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES state: \"Based on trial_instruction field (which port the animal should lick).\" The trajectory shows no discussion of using actual lick direction vs. instruction.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick direction choice*?\n\ni. Simple binary encoding: 'left' instruction maps to 0, 'right' instruction maps to 1. This per-trial value is broadcast across all 80 time bins in the output array.\n\nii.\n```python\nif instructions[ti] == 'left':\n    choice = 0\nelse:\n    choice = 1\noutput_choice.append(choice)\n# ...\nnp.full(N_BINS, output_choice[i], dtype=np.int64)  # broadcast per-trial\n```\n\niii. The AI treated choice as a per-trial variable broadcast across time, matching the instruction format.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from `intervals/trials/outcome` which contains string labels: 'ignore' (no response), 'miss' (wrong port), or 'hit' (correct).\n\nii.\n```python\noutcomes = np.array([o.decode() if isinstance(o, bytes) else o\n                    for o in f['intervals/trials/outcome'][:]])\n```\n\niii. The outcome field directly from the NWB trial table.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Outcome*?\n\ni. String-to-integer mapping: 'ignore' -> 0, 'miss' -> 1, 'hit' -> 2. This per-trial value is broadcast across all 80 time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nif outcomes[ti] == 'ignore':\n    outcome = 0\nelif outcomes[ti] == 'miss':\n    outcome = 1\nelse:  # hit\n    outcome = 2\noutput_outcome.append(outcome)\n# ...\nnp.full(N_BINS, output_outcome[i], dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Matches the decoder task specification: \"Outcome (ignore = 0, miss = 1, hit = 2, per-trial)\".\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Early lick* derived from?\n\ni. Binary encoding: 'early' maps to 1, everything else (including 'no early') maps to 0. Broadcast across all 80 time bins.\n\nii.\n```python\nel = 1 if early_lick[ti] == 'early' else 0\noutput_early_lick.append(el)\n# ...\nnp.full(N_BINS, output_early_lick[i], dtype=np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Derived from `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking/data` (shape N x 3, columns: x, y, likelihood) and `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking/timestamps`.\n\nii.\n```python\ntongue_ts = f['acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking/timestamps'][:]\ntongue_data = f['acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_TongueTracking/data'][:]\ntongue_y = tongue_data[:, 1]  # y-position\n```\n\niii. The AI identified the side-view camera tongue tracking from DeepLabCut, extracting the y-coordinate (column index 1).\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Tongue y-position*?\n\ni. For each trial: (1) extract tongue tracking data within the time window around the go cue, (2) align to go cue, (3) compute mean y-position per 50ms bin. No confidence/likelihood filtering is applied - all tracking data is used regardless of DLC confidence. Bins with no tracking frames get NaN.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef extract_tongue_y_for_trial(tongue_timestamps, tongue_y, go_cue_time, bin_edges):\n    n_bins = len(bin_edges) - 1\n    tongue_y_binned = np.full(n_bins, np.nan)\n    t_abs_start = go_cue_time + bin_edges[0]\n    t_abs_end = go_cue_time + bin_edges[-1]\n    idx = np.searchsorted(tongue_timestamps, [t_abs_start, t_abs_end])\n    ts_window = tongue_timestamps[idx[0]:idx[1]]\n    y_window = tongue_y[idx[0]:idx[1]]\n    ts_rel = ts_window - go_cue_time\n    for b in range(n_bins):\n        bin_mask = (ts_rel >= bin_edges[b]) & (ts_rel < bin_edges[b + 1])\n        if np.any(bin_mask):\n            tongue_y_binned[b] = np.mean(y_window[bin_mask])\n    return tongue_y_binned\n```\n\niii. The AI initially used a confidence threshold (likelihood >= 0.9) but found this produced ~80% NaN values and extreme class imbalance. It removed the filter, noting: \"All DLC tracking data used (no confidence filtering).\"\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session discretization: (1) collect all non-NaN tongue y values across all trials in the session, (2) compute 40th and 60th percentiles, (3) categorize: values below p40 -> 0, between p40 and p60 -> 1, above p60 -> 2. NaN bins default to category 1 (middle).\n\nii.\n```python\nall_tongue_y_vals = np.concatenate(tongue_y_all_trials)\nvalid_tongue_y = all_tongue_y_vals[~np.isnan(all_tongue_y_vals)]\np40 = np.percentile(valid_tongue_y, 40)\np60 = np.percentile(valid_tongue_y, 60)\nty_disc = np.ones(N_BINS, dtype=np.int64)  # default to middle\nty_disc[valid_mask & (ty < p40)] = 0\nty_disc[valid_mask & (ty >= p40) & (ty <= p60)] = 1\nty_disc[valid_mask & (ty > p60)] = 2\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES state: \"0 = below 40th percentile, 1 = 40th-60th percentile, 2 = above 60th percentile. Percentiles computed over all tongue y-position values across all trials in the session.\"\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Tongue y-position is binned into the same 80 time bins as the neural data using `BIN_EDGES`. For each bin, the mean y-position of all tongue tracking frames falling within that bin's time window is computed. The time bins are defined relative to the go cue, matching the neural alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\nfor b in range(n_bins):\n    bin_mask = (ts_rel >= bin_edges[b]) & (ts_rel < bin_edges[b + 1])\n    if np.any(bin_mask):\n        tongue_y_binned[b] = np.mean(y_window[bin_mask])\n```\n\niii. Alignment is achieved by using the same `BIN_EDGES` (relative to go cue) for both neural and tongue data.\n\n## 9. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several fallback/handling strategies: (1) Missing tone onset: falls back to -1.85s (typical value). (2) Missing tongue tracking in a bin: NaN, which defaults to category 1 (middle) after discretization. (3) Unparseable photostim values: caught by try/except, defaults to no photostim (all zeros). (4) Units with empty/unmappable brain region annotations: excluded. (5) Trials outside neural recording window: excluded. (6) Sessions with no good units: skipped. (7) String decoding: handles both bytes and str types for NWB string fields.\n\nii.\n```python\nif tone_onset_rel is None:\n    tone_onset_rel = -1.85  # fallback\n# ...\nty_disc = np.ones(N_BINS, dtype=np.int64)  # default NaN bins to middle category\n# ...\ntry:\n    ps_onset_val = float(ps_onset_str[ti])\nexcept (ValueError, TypeError):\n    pass  # defaults to zeros\n```\n\niii. The AI's trajectory shows it discovered and fixed several data issues: all-zero neural data for trials beyond recording window (fixed with obs_intervals check), output dtype float64 vs int64 (fixed), and tongue confidence filtering causing class imbalance (fixed by removing filter).\n\n## 10-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming steps are: (1) Loading spike times from NWB files - each file loads the entire `units/spike_times` array which can be very large. (2) Computing firing rates per unit per trial - this involves nested loops over units and trials, calling `np.histogram` for each combination. (3) Extracting tongue y-position per trial - another loop per trial with per-bin operations. The full conversion produced 144 sessions with ~75K trials.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Loading all spike times at once per session\nspike_times_data = f['units/spike_times'][:]\n# Nested loop: for each trial, for each unit\nfor ti in trial_indices:\n    for j, st in enumerate(unit_spike_times):\n        fr_matrix[j, :] = compute_firing_rates(st, gc, BIN_EDGES)\n```\n\niii. The trajectory shows the full conversion ran successfully. The conversion output doesn't include timing, but the nested unit x trial loop for firing rate computation is the clear bottleneck.\n\n## 10-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. Two main loops could be vectorized: (1) The inner loop over units for firing rate computation (line 492-493) could be replaced by vectorized binning across all units simultaneously for each trial. (2) The tongue y extraction loop over bins (lines 313-316) could be replaced with `np.digitize` or vectorized bin assignment. (3) The per-trial loop itself (line 487) processes one trial at a time but could potentially batch multiple trials.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Currently iterates per-unit:\nfor j, st in enumerate(unit_spike_times):\n    fr_matrix[j, :] = compute_firing_rates(st, gc, BIN_EDGES)\n\n# Currently iterates per-bin for tongue:\nfor b in range(n_bins):\n    bin_mask = (ts_rel >= bin_edges[b]) & (ts_rel < bin_edges[b + 1])\n```\n\niii. No justification given; the AI did not discuss vectorization opportunities.\n\n## 10-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. (1) String decoding from bytes - done separately for outcomes, early_lick, instructions, photostim_onset, photostim_duration, classification, and anno_names, each with the same pattern. (2) The `map_anno_name_to_region` function iterates through all region prefixes for each unit, even though many units share the same annotation. (3) Building `brain_regions` and `brain_region_idx` involves redundant `list.index()` lookups.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Repeated string decode pattern:\noutcomes = np.array([o.decode() if isinstance(o, bytes) else o for o in f['intervals/trials/outcome'][:]])\nearly_lick = np.array([e.decode() if isinstance(e, bytes) else e for e in f['intervals/trials/early_lick'][:]])\ninstructions = np.array([i.decode() if isinstance(i, bytes) else i for i in f['intervals/trials/trial_instruction'][:]])\n# ... same pattern repeated 7+ times\n```\n\niii. No justification given; this is a code style/efficiency observation rather than a correctness issue.\n\n## 10-d. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. (1) The observation intervals are loaded and processed for all units (including non-good units) before filtering - although the `min_obs_end`/`max_obs_start` computation only iterates over selected unit_indices. (2) All spike times for all units are loaded into memory even though only \"good\" units are used. (3) The per-trial output arrays broadcast scalar values (choice, outcome, early_lick) across all 80 time bins, creating 80 copies of the same value per trial - this wastes memory for per-trial variables. (4) The full tongue tracking data array is loaded into memory even if only a small portion overlaps with trial windows.\n\nii.\n```python\n# All spike times loaded, but only good units used:\nspike_times_data = f['units/spike_times'][:]  # loads ALL units\n# ...\nfor ui in unit_indices:  # only iterates over good units\n    start_idx = 0 if ui == 0 else spike_times_index[ui - 1]\n    end_idx = spike_times_index[ui]\n    unit_spike_times.append(spike_times_data[start_idx:end_idx])\n```\n\niii.
